morphism of topoi.

Fact 3.18

(Recollement of computability spaces)
.

Just as
3.17
shows that the topos of computability
spaces can be reconstructed from the induced open and closed subtopoi,
something similar can be said of each individual computability space. In
particular, for any
X\in\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textbf{{G}}}}
the following square is always cartesian:

X
\boldsymbol{j}_{*}\boldsymbol{j}^{*}X
\eta_{X}^{\boldsymbol{j}_{*}\boldsymbol{j}^{*}}
\boldsymbol{i}_{*}\boldsymbol{i}^{*}X
\boldsymbol{i}_{*}\boldsymbol{i}^{*}\boldsymbol{j}_{*}\boldsymbol{j}^{*}X
\eta_{X}^{\boldsymbol{i}_{*}\boldsymbol{i}^{*}}
\boldsymbol{i}_{*}\boldsymbol{i}^{*}\eta_{X}^{\boldsymbol{j}_{*}\boldsymbol{j}^{*}}
\eta_{bj_{*}\boldsymbol{j}^{*}X}^{\boldsymbol{i}_{*}\boldsymbol{i}^{*}}

The import of
3.18
is that it shows that any sheaf on

G
can be constructed entirely in terms of (left exact, idempotent)
monads on
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textbf{{G}}}}
without bringing either
\mathbf{Pr}\,{\boldsymbol{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathbf{M}}}
nor
\mathbf{Pr}\,{\boldsymbol{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathbf{R}}}

into the picture.

3.1.3
The internal language of computibility spaces

Although we will not expose them all in this paper, there are a number of
somewhat technical constructions of computability spaces that must ultimately
be carried out. As these constructions are
relative
in nature and must
constantly move between slices of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textbf{{G}}}}
, we may simplify things
considerably by recalling from
Section

1.1
that
type
theoretic internal languages
are the appropriate linguistic foundation for the
relative point of view.

It happens that
all
the constructions of
Section

3.1.2
are
stable under slicing, and can therefore be incorporated in a type theoretic
internal language. As a result, we may rephrase the results of

Section

3.1.2
as statements in the internal language of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textbf{{G}}}}

by adopting the following single postulate:

Postulate 1
.

There exists a proposition
\Phi
,
i.e
.
a type
\Phi
satisfying the condition
that every two of its elements are equal. We will write
{\medcirc}
for the
reflection of
\Phi
-modal types; we will write
{\medbullet}
for the reflection of

\Phi
-connected types. We additionally assume that
{\medcirc}{A}=A
strictly
when
\Phi=\top
.
6
6

6

This final assumption can be removed, but it is
convenient for our presentation.

Notation 3.19

(Subuniverses of modal types)
.

Given a universe
\mathcal{U}
, we will write
\mathcal{U}_{{\medcirc}}
and
\mathcal{U}_{{\medbullet}}

for the subuniverses spanned by
\Phi
-modal and
\Phi
-connected types
respectively. Note that unlike in univalent
foundations
(
Rijke et al. 2020
)
, it is not the case that

\mathcal{U}_{{\medbullet}}
is itself
\Phi
-connected nor that
\mathcal{U}_{{\medcirc}}
is

\Phi
-modal.

The language of type theory extended by
1
is referred to by

Sterling 2021
as
synthetic Tait computability
, because
it generates as if from the void an abstract form of Tait’s computability out
of the dynamics of
\Phi
-modal and
\Phi
-connected types in the internal
language, as these correspond under the computability spaces interpretation to
the syntactic components and their semantic instrumentations respectively.
Indeed, the internal / type theoretic version of the recollement of
computability spaces (
3.18
) is the following

3.20
, formally deducible in synthetic Tait
computability.

Observation 3.20

(Recollement of computability spaces, synthetically)
.

For any type
X
, the canonical “fracture function” defined below is an
isomorphism:

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle\to\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x_{0}:{\medcirc}{X}}}{\textstyle\sum_{x_{0}:{\medcirc}{X}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x_{0}:{\medcirc}{X}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x_{0}:{\medcirc}{X}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x_{1}:{\medbullet}{X}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\eta^{{\medbullet}}_{{\medcirc}{X}}x_{0}=_{{\medbullet}{\medcirc}{X}}{\medbullet}\eta^{{\medcirc}}_{X}x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x_{1}:{\medbullet}{X}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\eta^{{\medbullet}}_{{\medcirc}{X}}x_{0}=_{{\medbullet}{\medcirc}{X}}{\medbullet}\eta^{{\medcirc}}_{X}x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x_{1}:{\medbullet}{X}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\eta^{{\medbullet}}_{{\medcirc}{X}}x_{0}=_{{\medbullet}{\medcirc}{X}}{\medbullet}\eta^{{\medcirc}}_{X}x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x_{1}:{\medbullet}{X}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\eta^{{\medbullet}}_{{\medcirc}{X}}x_{0}=_{{\medbullet}{\medcirc}{X}}{\medbullet}\eta^{{\medcirc}}_{X}x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\eta^{{\medcirc}}_{X}x,\eta^{{\medbullet}}_{X}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\eta^{{\medcirc}}_{X}x,\eta^{{\medbullet}}_{X}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\eta^{{\medcirc}}_{X}x,\eta^{{\medbullet}}_{X}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\eta^{{\medcirc}}_{X}x,\eta^{{\medbullet}}_{X}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Notation 3.21
.

For any
\Phi
-modal type
A
, the map
k_{A}:A\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Phi\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Phi\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Phi\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Phi\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is invertible
by definition. We will permit the following abuse of notation: when
constructing an element of a
\Phi
-modal type
A
, we will write
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle a$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle a$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}
to
mean
k_{A}^{-1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\lambda{\_}.a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\lambda{\_}.a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda{\_}.a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda{\_}.a$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. Thus inside the delimiter, we
implicity bind a variable
\_:\Phi
.

Notation 3.22

(Extension types)
.

Let
A:\mathcal{U}
be a type and let
\_:\Phi\vdash a:A
a be partial element of
A
.
Then we shall write
A\mathrel{@}a
for the subtype
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\forall\_:\Phi.x=a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x:A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\forall\_:\Phi.x=a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\forall\_:\Phi.x=a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\forall\_:\Phi.x=a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
, called the
extension type
after

Riehl and Shulman 2017
.

Definition 3.23

(Vertical maps)
.

If
A
and
B
are types such that
{\medcirc}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A=B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A=B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A=B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A=B$}\mathclose{}{)}}
holds, then we define a

vertical map
from
A
to
B
to be a function of the form

f:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathrel{@}\lambda x.x
.

We refine the synthetic recollement of computability spaces
(
3.20
) with a special type connective to build
computability spaces from their
\Phi
-modal and
\Phi
-connected components.

Postulate 2

(Strict gluing
(
Gratzer et al. 2022
;
Sterling and Harper 2022
)
)
.

On any of the ambient universes
\mathcal{U}
, we have a
strict gluing
operation
that takes a
\Phi
-modal type
A:\mathcal{U}_{{\medcirc}}
and a family of

\Phi
-connected types
B:A\to\mathcal{U}_{{\medbullet}}
to a type

\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{Bx}:\mathcal{U}\mathrel{@}A
and an isomorphism
\mathsf{glue}_{A,B}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}Bx\cong\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{Bx}\mathrel{@}\pi_{1}
.

Notation 3.24

(Gluing projections and constructor)
.

For
g:\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{Bx}
, the first projection

\pi_{1}\mathsf{glue}_{A,B}^{-1}g
of
g:\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\operatorname{G}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{Bx}
can already be
written
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle g$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle g$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle g$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle g$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}:A
. We shall write
\underline{g}:B\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle g$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle g$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle g$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle g$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}
for the
second projection
\pi_{2}\mathsf{glue}_{A,B}^{-1}g
. Given
a:A
and
b:Ba

we shall write
a\lhd b
for the element
\mathsf{glue}_{A,B}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

can be internalized into the
synthetic Tait computability of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textbf{{G}}}}
by additional postulates.

Postulate 3

(The base model)
.

There is a
\Phi
-modal universe
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{Tp},\mathsf{El}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{Tp},\mathsf{El}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{Tp},\mathsf{El}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Tp},\mathsf{El}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
such that for each code

A:\mathsf{Tp}
the type
\mathsf{El}\,{A}
is
\Phi
-modal. Moreover,
\mathsf{Tp}
is closed under
function spaces as well as a base type
\mathsf{O}:\mathsf{Tp}
and two constants

\mathsf{yes},\mathsf{no}:\mathsf{El}\,\mathsf{O}
.

Postulate 4

(The model of variables)
.

There is an
additional decoding family
\mathsf{Var}
on
\mathsf{Tp}
such that for each
A:\mathsf{Tp}
, we
have
{\medcirc}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}\,{A}=\mathsf{El}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{Var}\,{A}=\mathsf{El}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{Var}\,{A}=\mathsf{El}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Var}\,{A}=\mathsf{El}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}
or equivalently
\Phi\Rightarrow\mathsf{Var}\,{A}=\mathsf{El}\,A
.

3.1.5
The computability space of normal forms

With
3
and
4
in hand, it becomes possible to
define a space of normal forms for types by means of an indexed inductive
definition — or, for the more categorically inclined, as the initial algebra
for a certain polynomial endofunctor on a slice of the ambient universe à la
Fiore
(
Fiore 2002
)
. In what follows, we will let
\mathcal{U}
be a sufficiently
large universe in the ambient type theory so as to classify each
\mathsf{El}\,A
and

\mathsf{Var}{A}
.

Definition 3.25
.

A
\mathcal{U}
-small
normal form algebra
is defined to be a series
of constants whose sorts we shall specify forthwith. First, a normal form
algebra requires a sort
\mathsf{NfTp}
of normal forms of types, and for each
type
A:\mathsf{Tp}
a pair of sorts
\mathsf{Nf}\,A,\mathsf{Ne}\,A
classifying

normal
and
neutral
forms of elements of
A
.

\mathsf{NfTp}:\mathcal{U}\mathrel{@}\mathsf{Tp}

\mathsf{Nf}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{Tp}}}{\textstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{Tp}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{Tp}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{Tp}}}\mathcal{U}\mathrel{@}\mathsf{El}\,A

\mathsf{Ne}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{Tp}}}{\textstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{Tp}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{Tp}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{Tp}}}\mathcal{U}\mathrel{@}\mathsf{El}\,A

Next we require constructors for the normal forms of each type:

\mathsf{nfO}:\mathsf{NfTp}\mathrel{@}\mathsf{O}

\mathsf{nfFun}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\textstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}\mathsf{NfTp}\mathrel{@}A\Rightarrow B

Finally we require constructors for neutral and normal forms of terms.

\mathsf{neVar}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\textstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{NfTp}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Var}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Var}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Var}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Var}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}\mathsf{Ne}\,A\mathrel{@}x

\mathsf{neApp}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\textstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{f:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{f:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{f:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{f:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}\mathrel{@}fx

\mathsf{nfNeO}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathsf{O}}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathsf{O}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathsf{O}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathsf{O}}}\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathsf{O}\mathrel{@}x

\mathsf{nfYes}:\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathsf{O}\mathrel{@}\mathsf{yes}

\mathsf{nfNo}:\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathsf{O}\mathrel{@}\mathsf{no}

\mathsf{nfLam}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\textstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{A,B:\mathsf{NfTp}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{f:\mathsf{Var}\,A\to\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{f:\mathsf{Var}\,A\to\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{f:\mathsf{Var}\,A\to\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{f:\mathsf{Var}\,A\to\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}\mathrel{@}\lambda x.fx

Definition 3.26
.

Let

from

is given by functions between

H_{\mathsf{NfTp}}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{NfTp}_{\mathfrak{M}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{NfTp}_{\mathfrak{M}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{NfTp}_{\mathfrak{M}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{A:\mathsf{NfTp}_{\mathfrak{M}}}}\mathsf{NfTp}_{\mathfrak{N}}\mathrel{@}A

that preserve all the operations of the normal form algebra in the sense of

Lemma 3.27
.

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mapsto A\mathrel{@}x
is an equivalence.

2

Lemma 3.28

\mathfrak{F}

3.1.6
Injectivity of normal type constructors

Let
\mathfrak{M}
be the initial normal form algebra.

Construction 3.29
.

Let
\mathsf{isFun}:\mathsf{NfTp}_{\mathfrak{M}}\to\mathcal{U}_{{\medbullet}}
be the family
sending each
A
to

{\medbullet}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathsf{NfTp}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{2}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle A=\mathsf{nfFun}_{\mathfrak{M}}\,B\,C$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathsf{NfTp}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{2}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle A=\mathsf{nfFun}_{\mathfrak{M}}\,B\,C$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathsf{NfTp}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{2}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A=\mathsf{nfFun}_{\mathfrak{M}}\,B\,C$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B,C$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathsf{NfTp}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{2}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A=\mathsf{nfFun}_{\mathfrak{M}}\,B\,C$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
.
We will define an auxiliary normal form algebra
\mathfrak{P}
such that

associates to each normal type

\mathsf{NfTp}_{\mathfrak{M}}
\mathcal{U}_{{\medbullet}}^{\to}
\mathcal{U}_{{\medbullet}}
\mathsf{cod}
\mathsf{isFun}

\mathsf{nfO}_{\mathfrak{P}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{nfO}_{\mathfrak{M}},\Phi,\lambda\_.\star$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{nfO}_{\mathfrak{M}},\Phi,\lambda\_.\star$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{nfO}_{\mathfrak{M}},\Phi,\lambda\_.\star$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{nfO}_{\mathfrak{M}},\Phi,\lambda\_.\star$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Fix
P
and

such that

\mathsf{nfFun}_{\mathfrak{M}}F=\mathsf{nfFun}_{\mathfrak{M}}Q
. Considering the

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{nfFun}_{\mathfrak{M}}Q,\top,\lambda{*}.\eta_{{\medbullet}}Q$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{nfFun}_{\mathfrak{M}}Q,\top,\lambda{*}.\eta_{{\medbullet}}Q$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{nfFun}_{\mathfrak{M}}Q,\top,\lambda{*}.\eta_{{\medbullet}}Q$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{nfFun}_{\mathfrak{M}}Q,\top,\lambda{*}.\eta_{{\medbullet}}Q$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Thus by projection, we have
\eta_{{\medbullet}}P=\eta_{{\medbullet}}Q
.
∎

3.1.7
The universe of normalization spaces

Our goal has been to define a natural model of type theory lying over the
bi-initial model
\mathbf{I}
in which normal forms can be projected from the
interpretations of types, following our discussion of
Tait 1967
in

Section

2.4.3
. Tait’s idea, which we will realize in a more technical form
here, is to let the semantic universe of the normalization model assign a
(vertical) projection map from each kind of semantic object into the
corresponding space of normal forms. In order to close such a universe under
function spaces, Tait noticed that it was necessary to have a vertical map

into
every semantic type from the space of neutral forms of elements of
that type.
In this section, we aim to define a universe of
normalization spaces
,
or computability spaces that are equipped with the structure described above.

Definition 3.31
.

A
normalization space

A
is given by the following data:

1.

a normal form
\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}:\mathsf{NfTp}
;

2.

a type
\mathsf{El}^{\#}{A}:\mathcal{U}\mathrel{@}\mathsf{El}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}
;

3.

a “reflection” map
\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}}}\mathsf{El}^{\#}{A}\mathrel{@}x
;

4.

a “reification” map
\mathord{\downarrow_{A}}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{El}^{\#}{A}}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{El}^{\#}{A}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{El}^{\#}{A}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{El}^{\#}{A}}}\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}\mathrel{@}x
.

Of course, the reflection and reification maps can be stated as a sequence of

vertical
maps
\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}\to\mathsf{El}^{\#}{A}\to\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}
.

Construction 3.32

(The universe of normalization spaces)
.

By
2
, we may define a type
\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}
of
normalization spaces such that
{\medcirc}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}=\mathsf{Tp}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}=\mathsf{Tp}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}=\mathsf{Tp}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}=\mathsf{Tp}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
strictly. Thus we
have a universe
\mathcal{N}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#},\mathsf{El}^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#},\mathsf{El}^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#},\mathsf{El}^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#},\mathsf{El}^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
that restricts under
{\medcirc}
to

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{Tp},\mathsf{El}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{Tp},\mathsf{El}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{Tp},\mathsf{El}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Tp},\mathsf{El}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

3.1.8
Closure of normalization spaces under connectives

We can close the universe of normalization spaces (
Section

3.1.7
)
under the connectives that we have postulated on
\mathsf{Tp}
in such a way that they
restrict exactly to these under
{\medcirc}
.

Construction 3.33

(The function space in normalization spaces)
.

For function spaces, we must define the following map (as well as
corresponding maps for
\lambda
-abstraction and application):

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Rightarrow^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Rightarrow^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}\times\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}\to\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}\times\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}\to\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}\times\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}\to\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}\times\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}\to\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathrel{@}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Rightarrow$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Rightarrow$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Given two normalization spaces
A,B:\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}
we must define a normalization
space
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}\mathrel{@}A\Rightarrow B
. Below, we
describe how to construct this space:

1.

To define the normal form
\mathord{\Downarrow}{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}}:\mathsf{NfTp}\mathrel{@}A\Rightarrow B
, we use the normal forms of
A
and of
B
to
construct
\mathsf{nfFun}\,{\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}}\,{\mathord{\Downarrow}{B}}
.

2.

To define the type
\mathsf{El}^{\#}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow^{\#}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}
over

\mathsf{El}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, we will use the function space
\mathsf{El}^{\#}{A}\to\mathsf{El}^{\#}{B}
. Note that this restricts only up to isomorphism to

\mathsf{El}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, but that this can be corrected using

2
. Therefore, we will not belabor the point
further in our informal explanation.

3.

To define the reflection map
\mathord{\uparrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}
, we are
given a neutral form
f:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and an element

x:\mathsf{El}^{\#}A
and must produce an element
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}x:\mathsf{El}^{\#}B\mathrel{@}fx
. Applying the reflection map for
B
, it
suffices to give a neutral form in
\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}\mathrel{@}fx
; applying
the neutral application constructor
\mathsf{neApp}
, we need only a normal
form in
\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}\mathrel{@}x
, why we obtain by reification at
A
.
All in all we have:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}x=\mathord{\uparrow_{B}}{\mathsf{neApp}\,{\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}}\,{\mathord{\Downarrow}{B}}\,{f}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\downarrow_{A}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\downarrow_{A}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\downarrow_{A}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\downarrow_{A}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

4.

To define the reification map
\mathord{\downarrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}
, we are given
a function
f:\mathsf{El}^{\#}{A}\to\mathsf{El}^{\#}{B}
and must exhibit a normal form

\mathord{\downarrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}f:\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathrel{@}\lambda x.fx
. Applying the normal abstraction constructor
\mathsf{nfLam}
, we are
given a variable
x:\mathsf{Var}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}
and must construct a normal form in

\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\displaystyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\textstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}{\mathopen{}{\lbag}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{\rbag}}\mathrel{@}fx
. Applying reification at
B
, it suffices to
give an element of
\mathsf{El}^{\#}{B}\mathrel{@}fx
; applying
f
itself, we need an
element of
\mathsf{El}^{\#}{A}\mathrel{@}x
which we obtain from reflection at
A
and
the neutral variable constructor
\mathsf{neVar}
. To summarize:

\mathord{\downarrow_{A\Rightarrow^{\#}B}}f=\mathsf{neLam}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{B}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\lambda x.\mathord{\downarrow_{B}}f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\lambda x.\mathord{\downarrow_{B}}f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda x.\mathord{\downarrow_{B}}f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda x.\mathord{\downarrow_{B}}f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\uparrow_{A}}\mathsf{neVar}\,\mathord{\Downarrow}{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

We leave the construction of
\lambda
-abstraction and application to the
reader, as they are automatic by the fact that
\mathsf{El}^{\#}A\to\mathsf{El}^{\#}B
is
itself a function space.

Construction 3.34

(The base type in normalization spaces)
.

For the base type, we must construct the following three constants:

\displaystyle\mathsf{O}^{\#}

\displaystyle:\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}\mathrel{@}\mathsf{O}

\displaystyle\mathsf{yes}^{\#}

\displaystyle:\mathsf{El}^{\#}\mathsf{O}^{\#}\mathrel{@}\mathsf{yes}

\displaystyle\mathsf{no}^{\#}

\displaystyle:\mathsf{El}^{\#}\mathsf{O}^{\#}\mathrel{@}\mathsf{no}

1.

We choose
\mathord{\Downarrow}{\mathsf{O}^{\#}}
to be
\mathsf{nfO}
.

2.

We will let
\mathsf{El}^{\#}\mathsf{O}^{\#}
be the type
\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathsf{O}
of normal
forms in the base type itself.

3.

The reflection map is given by
\mathsf{nfNeO}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathsf{O}}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathsf{O}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathsf{O}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:\mathsf{Ne}\,\mathsf{O}}}\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathsf{O}\mathrel{@}x
.

4.

The reification map given by the identity function.

Because we have chosen
\mathsf{El}^{\#}\mathsf{O}^{\#}=\mathsf{Nf}\,\mathsf{O}
, we may interpret

\mathsf{yes}^{\#},\mathsf{no}^{\#}
as
\mathsf{nfYes},\mathsf{nfNo}
respectively.

\mathsf{Tp}_{\mathbf{R}}

\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\gamma:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\gamma:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\gamma:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\gamma:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}}}\mathsf{Var}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle B\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle B\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\gamma,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\gamma,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mapsto\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle B\gamma,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle B\gamma,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B\gamma,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B\gamma,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

3.3.1
The functors of atomic and canonical points

Given a space
X\in\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textbf{{G}}}}
, a
atomic point
of
X
is defined to
be a generalized element of
X
defined on an atomic computability space
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}
;
likewise, a
canonical point
of
X
is defined to be a generalized
element of
X
defined on a canonical computability space
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
. Thus the

functors of (atomic, canonical) points
of
X
are the presheaves

\mathbf{Hom}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textbf{{G}}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}},X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}},X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}},X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}},X$}\mathclose{}{)}}
,
\mathbf{Hom}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textbf{{G}}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}},X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}},X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}},X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}},X$}\mathclose{}{)}}
respectively in

\mathbf{Pr}\,{\boldsymbol{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathbf{R}}}
.

.

The restriction

of any space

to its
functor of atomic points is canonically isomorphic to
X
itself.

Remark 3.47

(Toward an internal evaluation map)
.

The vertical map
\mathsf{Tp}
\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}_{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}}
internalizing the action
of
S
on types from
3.46
is a good first step,
what we
need
for our results is an unrestricted vertical map

\mathsf{Tp}
\mathsf{Tp}^{\#}
. We will do so by exhibiting for
any

X
a
canonical vertical map
X_{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}}
X
; recalling that
X\cong X_{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}}
, it evidently suffices to define a (suitably vertical)
natural transformation
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
from the functors of atomic
points to the functors of canonical points, which we shall refer in

Section

3.3.2
as
hydration
.

3.3.2
Hydration of variables via Bocquet, Kaposi, and Sattler’s
inserter

The goal of this section is to define a suitably vertical natural
transformation
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}{\mathopen{}{\llparenthesis}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrparenthesis}}

that “hydrates” an element of an

computability. Reindexing along this natural transformation, we would then

.

Construction 3.48

(The hydration model)
.

We choose
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathbf{H}}
to be the
inserter object

\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
is an
embedding, its direct image is (fully) faithful. As faithful functors reflect
monomorphisms, it suffices for us to show that
\boldsymbol{i}_{*}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{*}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is
a monomorphism in
\mathbf{Pr}\,{\boldsymbol{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathbf{R}}}
. Since
\boldsymbol{\rho}^{*}\cong\boldsymbol{i}^{*}\boldsymbol{j}_{*}
we have

\boldsymbol{i}_{*}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{*}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\cong\boldsymbol{i}_{*}\boldsymbol{i}^{*}\boldsymbol{j}_{*}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}={\medbullet}\boldsymbol{j}_{*}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. Hence it is enough to show that

{\medbullet}\boldsymbol{j}_{*}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow_{\mathbf{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a monomorphism.

Switching to the internal language, we suppress the embedding
\boldsymbol{j}_{*}
and aim
to check that the function
{\medbullet}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Rightarrow$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Rightarrow$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}\mathclose{}{)}}:{\medbullet}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{Tp}\times\mathsf{Tp}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{Tp}\times\mathsf{Tp}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{Tp}\times\mathsf{Tp}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Tp}\times\mathsf{Tp}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\medbullet}\mathsf{Tp}
is injective. Fixing
A,A^{\prime},B,B^{\prime}:\mathsf{Tp}

such that
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A^{\prime}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A^{\prime}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A^{\prime}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, our goal is to
check that
{\medbullet}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A^{\prime},B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. We know that

\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\Rightarrow B$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A^{\prime}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A^{\prime}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A^{\prime}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
; unfolding the definition of

\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}
induced by the normalization model in

3.50
and
3.51
we conclude that

\mathsf{nfFun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}A,\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}A,\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}A,\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}A,\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathsf{nfFun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}A^{\prime},\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}A^{\prime},\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}A^{\prime},\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}A^{\prime},\mathsf{norm}_{\mathsf{Tp}}B^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. Our goal then
follows from the modal injectivity of normal form constructors
(
Lemma

3.30
) together with our
3.52

that the normalization function is injective.
∎

4
Concluding remarks

We have at long last shown in
Theorem

3.54
how to prove that the
type constructor for function spaces is a monomorphism in the bi-initial model
of type theory with function spaces on a base type with two constants. A few
things deserve additional comment.

Extension to more sophisticated results

We have focused on the injectivity of ordinary function spaces for the sake of
simplicity, but the methods exposed herein also apply to dependent product,
dependent sums,
etc
. Likewise, our methods extend readily to prove more
difficult results, including the fact that the normalization function is not
only a section but in fact an isomorphism. From these results, one may deduce a
solution to the word problem for Martin-Löf type theory. Finally, these
methods can be adapted to apply to much more sophisticated type theories,
including cubical type
theory
(
Sterling 2021
;
Sterling and Angiuli 2021
)
, multi-modal type
theory
(
Gratzer 2022
)
, and even “
\infty
-type
theories”
(
Uemura 2022
)
.

Emphasis of universal properties over explicit constructions

At every stage in our development, we have worked as much as possible with
invariant universal properties rather than explicit constructions. For
instance, we worked with the (2,1)-categorical universal property of bi-initial
natural model not because we do not think that the concrete syntax of type
theory is important, but because we want our proofs to be flexible enough to
apply to
any
correct implementation of this concrete syntax,
i.e
.
any
presentation that can be shown to satisfy the universal property. The concrete
presentation of type theoretic syntax is both highly non-trivial and deeply
obscure: for this reason, it cannot be counted as a virtue for a proof to be
applicable only to a specific obscure presentation that is likely to be
superseded as the winds of fashion blow one way or another.

Likewise, it is possible to give an explicit construction of the “model of
variables” in terms of syntactically defined telescopes (see

Sterling 2021
for such a construction), but we have
followed the more modular proof technique of

Bocquet et al. 2021
not because we wish to worship abstraction
for abstraction’s sake, but because the proof applies to
any

presentation of the bi-initial model of variables. The flexibility to choose
different presentations is very important for implementation because such
choices can have a significant impact on efficiency; therefore, a modern proof
that is invariant in this way is arguably much closer to practical applications
than the more old-fashioned ones that emphasized explicit constructions.
